History
In 1372 DR, Fang, along with its masters, Methro, Jeth, Yage, and Sossi "Munroe", camped out on the outskirts of the hamlet of Liam's Hold along the Trade Way. The gang of vagrants quickly became a nuisance and danger to the community after they started harassing and roughing up local farmers. To guarantee the safety of her village, Mayor Wilsey listed the help of the individual known as the Hero of Daggerford. The adventurer was investigating mysteriously disappearing cattle on the Wild Pastures and was considered a trusted ally. Mayor offered the Hero the Stronghold - a small keep outside of the hamlet that once belonged to evil cultists of Bhaal, in exchange for driving the Monroe vagrants away without bloodshed.

## Farwatch Tower

Farwatch Tower was one of the major torchtowers of Waterdeep's Trollwall, located in the city's North Ward.Every tower connected to the Trollwall falls under the designation of "torchtower."The sourcebook City System states that all torchtowers with names are "major towers".

Location
This tower was located at the end of Tower March, just north of Watch Alley. Beneath the tower was one of the deep wells from which the city obtained its fresh, clean water.

Structure
Farwatch Tower stood five stories tall and was directly connected to the Trollwall that bounded the city and separated North Ward from the Field Ward.
Interior
Being a torchtower, Farwatch Tower had all the features that were common among them. This included a debriefing room, a small armory, holding cells, storage rooms for the holding of evidence or confiscated items, some bedchambers, and some garderobes. Additionally, being a major torchtower, it had six small holding cells that could hold up to ten people, which were used in times of trouble.

Behind an open doorway in the tower's southeastern wall, was a tall yet tiny bare sentry post with walls several feet thick, that was regularly monitored by the City Watch. This was because the sentry post was both haunted and contained a secret portal to Maldiglas’s Refuge. Despite their best efforts, it was often utilized by conspirators, lovers, and beggars seeking shelter.

Activities
Like all other torchtowers, this structure had three beacon-fires that were constantly kept on standby.

Inhabitants
Farwatch Tower, like all torchtowers, was manned by members of the City Watch.

## Faker's Crown

Faker's Crown was the enchanted decoy headdress that belonged to a king whose name was lost to time.

Powers
Faker's Crown carried several enchantments that enhanced the wearer's senses. While it was worn, it improved the wearer's hearing and allowed them to cast amplify and clairaudience/clairvoyance spells once per day each.

History
Even though the name of the king who commissioned this crown was lost to time, the story was not. The king ordered the creation of Faker's Crown after realizing that his advisers were plotting a coup. In secrecy, the king replaced the real crown with Faker's Crown just before the coronation. The enchanted headwear allowed the king to crack the conspiracy and capture the treasonous aides. Following that event, Faker's Crown became a supplemental symbol of regency.

Sometime later, a rogueish traveling bard swapped the fake and real crowns, leaving the Faker's Crown in the vault. The same night, she performed for the king. He was so mesmerized by the bard's music that he offered her anything from the castle's treasury. The woman claimed Faker's Crown and was never seen again.

By 1372 DR, the Faker's Crown fell into the hands of Feldran Exeltis of the Exeltis noble family, whose winery keep was located in the Blade Cliffs of the Sword Coast.

## Faldorn's Canticle: The Shadow Druid's Doctrine

Faldorn's Canticle: The Shadow Druid's Doctrine was a tome that collected the set of beliefs of the Shadow Druids, penned by Faldorn sometime before 1492 DR.

Description
Faldorn's Canticle was an old tome with its covers sprouting moss in the corners and was home to buzzing insects.

Contents
The Shadow Druid doctrine called for isolationism and aggression, claiming the balance of the nature was merely a myth.

History
In 1492 DR, a copy of this book was in possession of a druid named Kagha who was part of the Emerald Grove in the Western Heartlands. She secretly followed the Shadow Druid's Doctrine and hoped to perform the Rite of Thorns. Kagha's copy of the book was rugged by heavy use, its spine was softened and the book had a pared folded to mark a specific passage.

## Farfair River

The Farfair River was the largest river in the kingdom of Gnarhelm on the island of Alaron within the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
The Farfair River was located in eastern Gnarhelm near the border of Callidyrr. The river originated where the Lisbunt and Fairheight Mountains met. It flowed west then north around the base of the Lisbunt Mountains before reaching Lake Sunder's southern shore. The river continued on to the Sea of Swords, exiting from the lake's eastern shore near the coast. The Farfair River flowed for approximately  along its entire length.

## Farview

Farview was a small village located on the isle of Moray within the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
Farview was the westernmost settlement on Moray. It was located toward the center of the island on the coast of the Trackless Sea several miles west of the southern edge of the Breasal Marsh.

History
Northlander followers of the Storm Maiden liberated the village of Farview. They used timber harvested from the nearby Shannyth Forest to construct some of the finest ships seen within the Moonshae Isles. These ships were important because they allowed the Storm Maiden to move her troops among the islands of the Moonshaes, thus Farview was heavily guarded to ensure their protection.

## Fairheight

Fairheight was a county located in the kingdom of Callidyrr on the island of Alaron within the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
Fairheight was the smallest of Callidyrr's counties. It was located along the far northern border with Gnarhelm. The vast majority of the county was filled by the Fairheight Range and the mountain's foothills.

Government
The Masters of Fairheight were the Earls of the Blackstone family. They were among the most powerful nobles in Callidyrr as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Trade
The county was known as the center of mining and smelting in Callidyrr, making it a critical backbone of Callidyrr's economy.

History
In the, Earl Angus Blackstone betrayed High King Tristan Kendrick in exchange for favors from an agent of Talos. When his betrayal was discovered, he was slain and replaced as Earl by his son, Hanrold Blackstone.

Notable Locations
Several mining towns, including Blackstone, White Rock, and Highrock, were found in Fairheight. These settlements existed solely for the purpose of mining, and had to maintain large militias to protect themselves from the goblins and orcs of the mountains.

## Fallon

Fallon was a Nentyar hunter in . He betrayed the Nentyarch and kidnapped Araluen, intending to render her unto the Rotting Man; he eventually turned against his masters, but was slain in the process on Mirtul 7 of that same year.The event may also have happened in Mirtul 6, depending on whether midnight had fallen by the time the Rotting Man yielded in the fight against Araluen.

Description
While on garrison, Fallon wore dark green leather armor, and a silver leaf pin clasping a sea-green cloak.

Personality
Fallon was somewhat of a stickler for protocol who had a mild antipathy for Elowen, often going out of his way to annoy her. He was somewhat bothered by mention of the fall of Dun-Tharos. He also had an usually sour disposition, and a particular thirst for praise. He also had a bit of a covetous, envious streak. All of those seemed to stem from a certain egotism.

Though Anammelech had been the one to cultivate a relationship with him, he eventually came to serve the Rotting Man without any protest- albeit out of fear rather than loyalty.

However, he still had at his core a certain respect for life.

Abilities
Fallon was still a hunter trained by the Circle of Leth whose survival skills were superlative. He also knew of the way to employ the Keystone effectively, being able to use both created portals and allowing other influences into the Celestial Nadir.

Possessions
Of note, Fallon had been granted several special arrows by the Nentyarch.

Relationships
Anammelech had been Fallon's handler for some time. He went out of his way to annoy Elowen.

History
Before, Fallon was already Anammelech's ears in the Nentyarch's court, in exchange for gold.

Fallon was amongst the Yeshelmaar garrison that greeted Elowen when she arrived there alongside Araluen in . He attended the meeting with the Nentyarch the day after, where he was named one of the people that would accompany Araluen in her quest to confront the Rotting Man.

He suggested to Elowen's group that they meet at Yeshelmaar's Skymeadow as a distraction while he collected the keystone and Araluen, then took them to the Arches of Xenosi while the party was away; they barely managed to catch up to the door he opened into the Celestial Nadir. Fallon emerged on the Arches of Xenosi, where he rendered the keystone upon Anammelech, who set up an ambush and sent him on his way to the Rotting Man. He was contacted directly by the Rotting Man, telepathically, on Anammelech's death, and given orders to proceed through an underground route, as well as a magical compulsion to obey the Talontyr's orders.

While underground, in a moment of hesitation, the compulsion thereatened to destroy his mind; it was only Araluen's intervention that stopped it. Relieved, Fallon decided to switch sides again. He placed an ambush for one of Damanda's underlings in a hidden vault, easily slaying Ezekial. On reaching the surface, however, he was soon captured by Damanda; upon learning that Araluen's touch disintegrated vampires in a flash of light, she decided to keep him alive so that he could keep her moving. Nonetheless, he prepared a stake while they prepared to ambush the party coming after him.

When the party finally caught up to the zone where the vampires were ready for the ambush, Fallon cleverly clued them as to the fact he wasn't alone. He turned agains the vampires, however, just in time to prevent Lex from slaying Elowen; he staked her, but she retaliated just before disintegrating, slaying Fallon.

After his death, Fallon was redeemed by Araluen. She challenged the Rotting Man that same night, placing the day of his death as Mirtul 7.

## Farforevermore

Farforevermore was a rebellious subculture among nobles and the youth of wannabe-noble, rising merchant houses, in both the cities of Athkatla and Waterdeep in the late 14 century DR. Those who were part of the subculture were referred to as "Forevers".

Activities
This subculture involved adopting a persona in which one dressed and acted in the ways that a city's nobility had done a century prior, such as old-fashioned courting or the keeping (and even flogging) of slaves. When accused of playacting, most Forevers would hotly deny the accusation and insist that they were improving both themselves and their city, referring to the nobiltiy of the prior century as having "better" ways of life.

Forevers often maintained hidden rooms or private attic spaces, using them to change from their everyday clothes to that of their persona. They also tended to pool funds together with other Forevers in order to host revels or rent out mansions where they could carry out their playacting.

## Faenasuor

Faenasuor was an ancient garrison city of the sea elf kingdom of Aryselmalyr, as well as one of the major sea elf colonies of the Sea of Fallen Stars in the 14 century DR.

Description
The structures of this city were heavily encrusted in coral. It also held some of the most pristine and representative pieces of ancient Aryselmalyn art.

Geography
Faenasuor was located on the continental slopes east of Starmantle, approximately  north of that surface city, in an area that solely the sea elves referred to as the Lost Lands.

History
Faenasuor was originally founded in the year  by the kingdom of Aryselmalyr, albeit under another name that by the 14 century DR had long since been lost to time. In the year, the city was abandoned and over time it was buried by sea storms under tons of detritus and silt.

Around the 13 century DR, this garrison city was rediscovered and exacted by the High Mages of Sylkiir. By the year, it was officially resettled by sea elves and other students of history and was given the new name of Faenasuor. Many of these sea elves who settled at the city sought to profit off of the abundant pearl beds that surrounded it.

## Favil Blanthe

Exchequer Favil Blanthe was the financial manager of the Flaming Fist in the 15 century DR.

Description
The exchequer was a corpulent senior citizen of the Gate.

Personality
Blanthe, surprisingly, lacked discipline and did not care much for the law in comparison to his comrades. He belived that the Guild existed due to the terrible social injustices and class system of Baldur's Gate.

Activities
As exchequer, Blanthe was most often found at the Blade and Stars or the Counting House. Though formerly a member of the Guild, the financial manager took up the worship of Torm, the Loyal Fury.

History
Around the 1420s DR, Blanthe was born in Rivington and later became a member of the Guild. Here, he operated under the alias, "Gold Lahar", and became a formidable bookmaker and burglar until around the 1440s DR. Due to a dispute, Lahar wished to leave the Guild in order to avoid being killed. It was then that he took on the name "Favil Blanthe" and assumd a new identity.

After his former associates believed that he had fled or otherwise, he signed up with the Flaming Fist. During his first half a year, all Guild members who were associated with Gold Lahar had disappeared. Favil quickly worked his way up the ranks of the Flaming Fist, in part due to his inside knowledge of the Guild. Due to his service, he became a spymaster for the Flaming Fist, and later the Exchequer.

Blanthe's life would get more difficult when Rilsa Rael came to find out his former identity. Around 1482 DR, she wished to stock up on weapons and armor to equip the Guild, and blackmailed Blanthe in order to make this happen.

Rumors & Legends
It was rumored that he heavily indulged in drinking brews of a dwarven nature.

## Fang of Talona

Fangs were specialty priests of the Church of Talona who worshiped the Lady of Poison through both healing and harming.

Activities
Fangs used disease and poisons to harm others, but also used their abilities to make antidotes and heal as well.

Culture
Most fangs voluntarily scarred their faces and covered their bodies in tattoos to honor Talona. Fangs laced their words with veiled threats, taking advantage of their goddess' aura of fear in order to further their goals.

Abilities
Given their close work with poisons and venoms, fangs were better able to resist their effects. These substances also took longer to affect a fang, giving them more time to prepare an antidote. All fangs were skilled at brewing poisons and their appropriate antidotes using specific methods which developed over time.

Fangs were immune to all diseases both normal and magical, save for lycanthropy. They could also cure disease in other several times per week.

They were slow to engage in normal combat, preferring instead to backstab or use poison. Fangs could not turn undead. They also found the undead to be especially disgusting since their poisons had no effect on them, making them much more awkward if forced to combat any form on undead with weapons.

Possessions
Fangs dressed in drab olive-colored clothing. A black chain worn around their neck bore their holy symbol. Leather armor was the only armor they would consider wearing, and this was always dyed green or black. They preferred using weapons that were easy to use with poison including darts, blowpipes, daggers, and short swords.

## Favored

The favored were specialty priests of the Church of Tymora who were tasked by Lady Luck to provide good luck to adventurers.

Activities
These individuals spent most of their time adventuring, providing Tymora's good graces to their companions, or random strangers they met while exploring, at the same time.

Culture
Confidence surrounded the favored and rightly so, given that Tymora's blessings were indeed bountiful with them. They did not worry about the future, even when appearing to behave recklessly, because their goddess held their fate in her hands.

Abilities
The favored experienced good luck so often that it appeared to be a special ability of theirs. All favored could turn undead.

Possessions
The favored wore whatever armor they wished and wielded the weapon of their choice. They appeared to be normal adventurers aside from their holy symbol and a permanent smirk that rarely left their face.

## Farshore Straits

The Farshore Straits, or simply the Farshore, was a thin marine passage between the island of Ilighôn and the Vilhon Reach mainland.

Geography
The Farshore ran south of Ilighôn, in a similar fashion to the Paddle Straits, located south of the isle of Wavecrest. There were two entrances into the Vilhon Reach form the Sea of Fallen Stars: through the central part of the Eyes of Silvanus, sailing "between the eyes of Silvanus" as they said – the islands of Ilighôn and Wavecrest. The second entrance was through the risky waters of the Farshore Straits. Smaller vehicles preferred to enter through the Farshore due to its narrow size. Pirate and smuggling vessels often took advantage of large ships, rarely risking the straits' jagged rocks.

Despite common use by undesirable parties, the Farshore Straits were monitored from the Aphrunn Mountains, which offered a wide-open overview of the aquatic passageway.

## Fallen Temple

The Fallen Temple, also known as the Temple of the Broken Hammer, was a cult once devoted to Tyr responsible for many crises in the Utter East until the late 14 century.

History
Originally, the Fallen Temple was a branch of the Church of Tyr, led by Patriarch Justarvis, High Avenger of Tyr's Church of the Southern Clave of the Moonshaes, that accompanied the Ffolk in their migration from the Moonshae Isles to the Utter East alongside branches of the Church of Umberlee, the Church of Chauntea, the Church of Tempus, and the Church of Waukeen.

But Patriarch Justarvis allied himself with Count Boarswic, the Northmen, the followers of Tempus, who, when their ships were struck with plague and turned away by each port and town, convinced him to get retribution. Soon, Justarvis and his followers began to kill, loot, and raze, though they labeled their deeds righteous and their victims deserving of punishment. Tyr didn't see it that way and punished Justarvis with a horrible doom, as a kraken grabbed him and his guards and pulled them under the sea, just hours before the Ffolk's Right Armada landed in Utter East.

The heretic Tyrran priests who followed him faced twin punishments, both secular and divine. First, they were given no right to vote at the final Great Council and when the Founding Lords carved up the Utter East between themselves, they granted the Tyrrans no fiefs of their own, neither individually nor collectively. Second, Tyr himself forbade them all holy powers and spells.

Thus, the resentful heretics looked for a new source of divine power, and in time their descendants devoted themselves to the mysterious Ysdar, an old and wicked power of the Outer Planes that had based itself in the Utter East and desired the destruction of all life there. The Fallen Temple preyed on everyone in all the Five Kingdoms, and in time it even managed to claim its own bloodforge, either by digging one up or seizing it from someone.

The cultists plagued the Utter East, gathering their new adepts among the conquered Mar. They had many cells in every city and all cultists received their orders during their dreams.

In the, the personal agent of Emperor Aetheric III of Doegan, the Mar psion chancellor Ikavi Garkim managed to defeat single-handedly a cell based in his old quarter in Eldrinpar.

Despite it being his doing, Emperor Aetheric III convinced the group of paladins led by Miltiades of Phlan that the culprits behind the abduction of Eidola of Neverwinter, the soon-to-be wife of Piergeiron Paladinson, were members of the Fallen Temple. Then the emperor asked the paladins to defeat once for all the Fallen Temple, and after a communion with their deity Tyr, they agreed to destroy the enemies of Doegan.

Afterward, Artemis Entreri and his mercenaries managed to injure Aetheric III and the magical barriers around Eldrinpar failed, flooding the city with fiendish monsters, hordelings, and tanar'ri. Seizing advantage, the cultists managed to steal the bloodforge of Doeagan and secretly gained control of the Temple of Umberlee, killing all the local priests there.

They planned to use the two bloodforges they had obtained to finally fully summon Ysdar into the Utter East. However they were attacked at the last moment by a mixed group of both paladins and mercenaries. In the process, the deity Tyr revealed himself through his new Chosen—the blind boy mercenary Ingrar Welven. The deity manifested himself through ominous red skies and collapsed part of the temple's dome. Disgusted by the cult that claimed to be his worshipers, Tyr incinerated many, and forced the survivors to flee.

## Falling Star Tavern

The Falling Star Tavern was a drinking establishment located in the port city of Khaerbaal in Halruaa.

Atmosphere
The rowdy tavern was located in the city's docks next to a busy fishery. The tavern was popular spot among the young men in training of the House Jordain to blow off steam in bar brawls. In contrast to pugilism, verbal battles were just as common as debating was one of the jordaini's many skills. Among other entertainment provided by the drinking establishment were regular bardic performances. However, they did not take place daily.

Members of the Halruaan navy, adorned in blue-green uniforms, and various local militia groups, were a big share of the Falling Star's clientele. Pirates frequented the establishment, but they tended to keep a low profile. As duels were popular in Khaerbaal, folk tended not to poke their noses in other people's personal business. And in case duels did happen, the Falling Star kept the alley behind the tavern free of garbage and debris.

Merchants, blacksmiths, dwarven miners, and many other locals could be seen imbibing the tavern's liquid dinner. Halruaan golden wine was a popular drink, served in silver decanters.

History
In the, young jordains Matteo and Themo visited the tavern after a murder of a classmate by Halruaan Inquisitrix Kiva. Themo started a big tavern brawl to vent out grief and frustration, while Matteo subdued his Northman friend. Matteo paid for the damages and quickly befriended a Halruaan house mage after buying him a jug of wine.

## Favor of Ilum Lura

Favor of Ilum Lura was a set of unique enchanted gloves that dated back to the ancient Illefarn.

Powers
Favor of Ilum Lura significantly enhanced the wearer's dexterity.

History
These magic gloves originated from Nivian, a city in Illefarn. In, the champion of Nivian's Artisan's District was Beloran. He was madly in love with the ruler's niece, the beautiful Ilum Lura. Beloran remained the champion for the next six years, clamoring for the woman's hand through becoming the city's Grand Champion. Ilum's father, impressed with the champion, commanded his daughter to gift Beloran with a traditional token of favor. She presented the champion with a pair of enchanted riding gloves. In the following championship, Beloran fought even fiercer than before as the tradition dictated that the token's presenter was to reward the champion with a kiss. Ilum reluctantly kissed Beloran but revealed that her heart belonged to another. Consumed by maddening grief, Beloran left Nivian forever, still wearing the Favor of Ilum Lura.

As of the, a merchant of Port Llast, Haljal Throndor, owed a vast collection of enchanted items in his special stock.

## Fallowfern Lane

Fallowfern Lane was an angled southbound street in the walled town of Mosstone that stood along the Trade Way in Tethyr.

Geography
Sairnor Street merged with the southern end of the Fallowfern Lane at a steep angle, forming the distinct address where Taluth's Shrewd Flight, an archery store, could be found. Another location of some note that stood on the crossing of the two streets was a forgettable hot-bun shop.

## Faerdaleeyn

Faerdaleeyn was a member of the Ala-min tribe within the Lake of Steam region during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Though scarred from many battles, Faerdaleeyn was still considered handsome in a rugged way. He had deep black eyes that often intimidated strangers.

Activities
Faerdaleeyn was the elder of the Ala-min as of the . He inherited leadership upon the death of his father, Zalaster.

Unbeknownst to him, an agent of the Harpers named Frederique hoped to use Faerdaleeyn and his tribe to attack a group of Banites who travelled through their lands.

## Faren Starlight

Faren Starlight was a Zhentarim enchanter who was the "keeper" of the black dragon Harondalbar during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Description
Faren was a very attractive woman of average height. She had long black hair and emerald green eyes. Her skin was pale to the point of giving her a near ethereal appearance.

Activities
Faren excelled at her role since Harondalbar was somewhat fascinated with her. Given the dragon's often stubborn nature and refusal to rouse to confront any non-Zhentarim caravans moving through Yellow Snake Pass, Faren often resorted to creating her own illusions of Harondalbar to warn off unwanted traffic.

Possessions
Faren carried a staff of power, bracers of defense, a brooch of shielding, and a pair of winged boots.

Abilities
She usually had the following spells memorized – burning hands, charm person, color spray, comprehend languages, detect magic, feather fall, sleep, shocking grasp, spider climb, phantasmal force, alter self, bind, deafness, fog cloud, insatiable thirst, summon swarm, Melf's acid arrow, Tasha's hideous laughter, explosive runes, hold person, lightning bolt, spectral force, and suggestion.

Relationships
Faren was an apprentice of both Manshoon and Sememmon.

Faerndel was a mage of Mourktar during the mid-3 century DR. He was offered the position of the Magister by Azuth, but turned it down, being the first recorded mage to refuse the position.

## Faerdinalar

Faerdinalar the Enchanter was a famous mage sometime on or before the late 13 century DR. He was once the master of two different Magisters: Thornar Fleetmoor and Uldinus Lawkland.

## Faerglamer House

Faerglamer House was the residence of Bilnur Faerglamer through the late 9 and throughout the 10 century DR.

Location
The house was in Tethyr.

Interior
During Bilnur's stay, the house was inhabited by him alone, with piles of coins abandoned across the floor; birds nested freely inside the abandoned rooms. The outside of the house remained overgrown and somewhat dilapidated. After he died, the house slowly turned into a ruin, broken open to the sky.

Activities
Bilnur Faerglamer lived there and used it as a workshop.

Defenses
While Bilnur lived there, he had a number of guardians, probably variants of helmed horrors with more power than normal; those melted apart after his death.

History
Prior to the, the house had been built for a then-extinct family. Bilnur Faerglamer lived in the house from around, the better part of seventy years prior to, to, the year of his death. After his death, the house decayed into a ruin, with the coinage stashed there soon taken.

Rumors & Legends
Though the coins that Bilnur hoarded had been taken long since, some mages of the late 14 century DR still expected to find a cache somewhere in the ruined and overgrown manor. However, there were rumors of often invisible shapechangers hunting visitors to the ruins.

Inhabitants
Bilnur Faerglamer

## Falhnen Battlescar

Falhnen Battlescar was a fighter and a cleric from the town of Longsaddle, active in the 14 century DR.

History
Falhnen hailed from the small town of Longsaddle, just outside Neverwinter Wood. He was a lowly descendant of peasants, who, in turn, were descendants of slaves who settled in the Savage Frontier. Despite his low social standing, by the age of twenty-one, Falhnen Battlescar fell in love with Rachel Del Goldwhite, a princess daughter of a local noble family. Her father swore to kill Falhnen if he ever crossed the nobleman's line of sight. This was the drive behind Falhnen Battlescar's adventuring career. He sought to gain money and status to change the mind of Rachel's father.

Sometime in the 14 century DR, Falhnen Battlescar was among the ambitious fortune seekers who were interested in being sent into a mysterious dungeon by an unnamed sorceress to retrieve the equally mysterious magical orb from its depths.

Fallen Stars Fish was a fresh fish shop located in the city of Waterdeep during the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Location
The shop was located in the southwest section of Waterdeep's North Ward on the south side of Suldown Street.

Activities
All of the fish sold here came from the Sea of Fallen Stars. Many customers were willing to pay the high prices for cleaned and gutted fish here because it was rumored that the owner used magic to ensure they were only several hours old after being caught and delivered.

Gatos Firlack owned Fallen Stars Fish.

## Falar Aporos

Falar Aporos was a criminal in Calaunt.

Description
He was young, lanky and clean-shaven. His hair was curly, and his face was foxy. He wore a finely stitched and embroidered cloak, jerkin and breeches.

Personality
Falar was thought to be always looking for an advantage.

Possessions
Falar Aporos had at least one sword. He also employed a sling.

History
Falar Aporos rescued Taegan Nightwind from an ambush.  Afterwards, they discussed his fate, and Taegan volunteered to steal Arveene Dalurieth, whom Falar described as his beloved, from the tower where her father kept her.

However, Falar was actually behind the ambush, which he used as cover to kill Vonn Dalurieth's dire bear and steal the other with his men. Taegan Nightwind found him days later hiding in a warehouse with the bear. There was a fight, and Falar was killed by the stolen bear during it.

The shop faced the Tower of the Royal Magician across the Promenade and neighbored a merchants' drinking club called Bindle's.

## Far Realm battery

A Far Realm battery was a device that could store energy―including raw magic―that had been extracted from the Far Realm.

Description
It resembled an amethyst crystal, set within an elongated cylinder crafted from glass and metal. They measured about  long and  in diameter.

When activated, it would emit a -long beam of light.

Powers
The Far Realm batteries could be used to power permanent portals, and other devices that utilized one or more forms of energy, including powerful explosives.

History
Some time during or after the, Red Wizards of Thay established a hizagkuur-reinforced Far Realm battery factory in the ruins of Xorvintroth, beneath the snowy wastes of Icewind Dale.

Notable Owners
 Jaan'vald, a githyanki freedom fighter, once adapted a Far Realm battery with "mimic protoplasm". This created a mimic battery that could power just about any device to which it was attached.

## Fallenfalcon

Fallenfalcon was a tiny community of several families that existed around a camping pavilion in the wild realm of Owlhold in the Border Kingdoms.

Description
Fallenfalcon could be reached by traveling north along the Bowshot from the lone Griffon Aflame inn. The entirety of the thorp consisted of four rustic cottages that were the homes of half-elven families. The main attraction for those traveling the untamed land was a large camping pavilion and a small spring, which was the source of clean drinkable water. The cottages were built around a pool connected to the spring.

The families of the two cottages closest to the pavilion eagerly greeted travelers, offering them bread, soup, or a bigger meal if ordered ahead of time.

The lands north of Fallenfalcon were flush with various herbs and berries, often gathered along the trails that ran through the wilderness.

Notable Inhabitants
Jlonkin the Bowyer and his family inhabited one of the closest cottages to the road and pavilion in the late 14 century DR.
Nressa the Weaver and her family lived in the other of the two cottages closest to the Bowshot road in the late 14 century DR.

## Falls of Tumbling Stars

The Falls of Tumbling Stars was a waterfall and hidden mountain valley in the Thunder Peaks north of Cormyr. It was believed sacred to Mielikki, the Lady of the Forest, and most significant site of the church of Mielikki in the late 14 century DR.

Geography
The falls stood on the Immerflow river just after its headwaters in the Twin Peaks of Urlspur. The Immerflow ran from north-east to south-west and cascaded down the mountainsides, where it met the long East Way linking Sembia and Cormyr before it continued into the Hullack Forest. The whole valley was concealed from the wider world and could only be reached via secret trails.

Description
Within the vale were a number of holy pools. Anyone who was wounded and bathed in these would, by Mielikki's grace, be miraculously restored.

Inhabitants
The vale was home to a small community of human and half-elf Walkers of the Forest Way, including highly skilled rangers and priests of Mielikki.

Government
Circa 1370 DR, the Mielikkian community here was led jointly by Hawk of the Lady Neretheen Jalassan, a priestess, and by Hawk of the Lady Beldryn Stormstone, who was Lord Ranger.

Defenses
The valley was well-defended against trespassers by Mielikkian rangers and archers, who all had lethal prowess with the bow.

Activities
The Mielikkians would escort their fellow faithful and injured people in need of healing to the vale via the secret trails. It was also a setting off point for high-ranking members of the church to make pilgrimages to the far-off Unicorn's Run.

They were also the greatest enemies of the Bloodmoon Circle of Malarites based at the Wyvernstones of Hullack. In this, they were joined by members of the church of Eldath operating in the area.

## Fatzon Axemaiden

Fatzon Axemaiden was a dwarven fighter and once, a member of the Hearth Guard, active in the 14 century DR.

History
Fatzon was a fierce warrior as strong if not stranger than any male dwarf. She hailed from the Dwarven Valley of the Kelvin's Cairn in frigid Icewind Dale. By the age of sixty-four, Fatzon left the valley in self-imposed exile. She was trained as a Hearth Guard, one of the dwarven defenders, and at some point in Axemaiden's past, she failed her sworn duty to defend the family holding. Each slain monster or opponent brought Axemaiden one step closer to avenging the deaths of her family.

Sometime in the 14 century DR, Fatzon Axemaiden was among ambitious adventurers who were interested in being sent to a mysterious dungeon by an unnamed sorceress to retrieve the equally mysterious magical orb from its depths.

## Fang of Yeenoghu

A Fang of Yeenoghu was a gnoll that had been been possessed by a demonic spirit, a gift bestowed upon them by the demon lord Yeenoghu. They were considered the Lord of Savagery's most favored servants.

Abilities
Whenever a Fang of Yeenoghu slew another creature, the victim's corpse could in a sense birth new gnolls. Any hyena that feasted upon the remains of a Fang's prey would initiate a horrific transformation. The hyena's body would burst open, and a fully-grown mature gnoll would emerge from its viscera. This frightful metamorphosis reflected the moment when the first gnolls were spawned by Yeenoghu himself.

Combat
Fangs of Yeenoghu often wielded 'cudgels of bloody teeth', massive greatclubs studded with teeth that seeped blood.

Society
Yeenoghu's chosen garnered further favor from the demon lord by capturing mortals as slaves and dragging them down to his domain in the Abyss. They often served as shamans that presided over rituals dedicated to the Prince of Gnolls.

Notable Fangs of Yeenoghu
 Cyhzka, the Fang that spawned the gnolls of the Cackling Chasm.
 Krurg Mucusface, one of Yeenoghu's servants who was captured on the infernal battlefield of Avernus and taken prisoner.

## Falling wall (divine)

Falling wall was a spell that could weaken a wall.

Effects
During the length of the spell, the caster had to remain in physical contact with the targeted wall. The spell only weakened masonry, mortar and stone. The damage inflicted by the spell was the equivalent of several years worth of exposure to the elements. The spell could affect an area of up to . This area grew as the caster became more skilled.

Components
This spell required verbal and somatic components.

Falling wall was one of the spells found in Leaves of Green, a divine spellbook and a holy book of Silvanus.

## Favored of Mielikki

A favored of Mielikki was a follower of the Forest Queen who was especially faithful to her cause and was blessed by her in turn.

Culture
Such folk had loved the wilds for the whole of their lives, from its grandest landscapes to its tiniest wonders. They felt an instant attraction to the faith of Mielikki, describing it as like reuniting with an old friend.

Activities
They worked hard to serve the goals of the Forest Queen, namely achieving harmony with nature, protecting the forest and the wider world, and upholding natural laws, while teaching their knowledge to others and inspiring them to follow their ways.

Prerequisites
They needed to be good of heart and faithful followers of Mielikki.

Abilities
In battle, thanks to Mielikki's good graces, the favored could aid their allies to act swiftly when needed. Moreover, they were not hindered by plant cover, shadow, or anything else that obscured sight when attacking a foe outside. They could more easily seriously harm both natural and unnatural creatures in Mielikki's judgement—beasts, plant creatures, and aberrant creatures—also daze those they did serious harm too.

Mielikki granted her favored a few magical powers based around plants and forest. They could conjure protective vines to ensnare and constrict their enemies, and thereby protect their allies, up to  away. They could glimpse and even use the magical pathways that only beings like dryads used to treestride, thus teleporting between any two large plants (or plant creatures) up to  once a day. Finally, they could call on the forest itself once a day to cause plant life to grow thick and green over an area up to  wide. It blocked sight, was difficult to traverse, and tripped, ensnared, and constricted enemies, while boosting the fighting prowess of allied beasts and plant creatures.

Appendix
References

## Faerie flames

Faerie flames was a very rare spell used by elven clerics.

Effects
This spell was a more powerful variant of faerie fire. When cast, this spell, like faerie fire, outlined a target in a fiery glow. However, this spell inflicted damage to the targeted creature or object. The flames could not be extinguished like regular fire, so dousing in water or smothering the target had no effect. The spell was vulnerable to dispel magic and magical means of extinguishing fire such as a wand of flame extinguishing.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as material components  of sulfur and a piece of foxfire. The material components were consumed by the spell during casting.

Appendix
References

---

## Faith arrow

Faith arrow was a spell used by elven clerics, primarily the clergy of Corellon Larethian, Rillifane Rallathil, Solonor Thelandira.

Effects
This spell allowed the caster to enchant several mundane arrows, imbuing them with divine power. The amount of arrows that could be enchanted was determined by the caster's skill level, up to six arrows. The spell had no effect on magical arrows. As long as the target was within range and sight, the enchanted arrows never missed. The arrows could not penetrate material that stopped a mundane arrow, but could follow a target. When the arrow struck its target, it delivered devastating damage. If the arrows passed through a dead-magic zone or an anti-magic shell, it lost its enchantment. Once enchanted, an arrows remained enchanted until it was used, afterwards it turned to dust.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required material components, the holy symbol of the caster and the arrows. The arrows had to have been crafted by an expert fletcher.

Appendix
References

## Faragati

Faragati was an half-elven adventurer hailing from a big city who ventured to Vaasa sometime before 1372 DR.

History
One day Faragati came across a young and good-looking elven lass meditating in a secluded brook during his travels across Vaasa. He was instantly mesmerized, and the woman invited him to join. Her name was Parley, and as she meditated, Faragati sat beside her, unable to look away from the serene beauty. When the mediation was over, Faragati and Parley spoke at length about their lives, adventuring, and the wonders the world had to offer. Unfamiliar with the city life, druid Parley inquired about such an alien locale. She expected Faragati to be negative about his old home, but he claimed the city had its own unique beauty. After the two parted ways, Faragati's words inspired Parley to travel and see the world, becoming an adventurer.

## Faze

Fazes were massive, rare aberrations known to live in the Astral Plane.

Description
Fazes resembled jellyfish, with translucent, purplish domes concealing the internal organs, and six white tentacles hanging below. The skin was tough and rubbery. Fazes were typically considered exotically beautiful when seen from a distance.

Behavior
Fazes were, unfortunately, evil creatures that preferred to attack good creatures.

Abilities
Fazes' most dangerous ability was how they fed and what they ate, for they drained intelligence from sentient creatures within . This draining was not easily noticeable by most creatures. If a creature passed out because of the drain, fazes were known to eat their victims.

Fazes were also capable of of the psionic powers daze monster, feather fall, and shatter, each usable thrice per day.

Combat
Fazes preferred to feed on good creatures, but if they went a week or more without eating, they would attack any sentient creature. However, since their intelligence drain was not always noticed by their victims, fazes preferred to eat without actually entering combat. If their feeding continued to go unnoticed, they would eat more than strictly necessary until their victims fell unconscious. If it was noticed, however, and the faze was threatened, it would retreat as soon as it was full.

Ecology
Fazes were found, though rarely, in the Astral Plane, but it was unknown whether they were actually native to that plane. There they drifted along in search of food and nothing more, much like oceanic jellyfish. They were not known to speak, not even telepathically like other psionic aberrations. They preyed upon any creatures they ran across; consequently, githyanki would kill fazes on sight.

For an unknown reason, fazes congregated near Crosswinds Keep, whose inhabitants kept them away with spells and siege weapons.

Appendix
References

Most faernor were skilled healers and herbalists.

## Fanged Mirror of Yehcir-Eya

The Fanged Mirrors of Yehcir-Eya were a set of thirty-three magical artifacts capable of manifesting a vicious reflection of whoever gazed upon them.

Description

The Fanged Mirrors were small, circular pieces of silvered glass framed by golden, sweeping fangs.

Powers
The primary function of the Fanged Mirrors was to render physical the soul of its victim. Without fail, this manifested soul would then aggressively attempt to murder its owner. Although historically most victims of the Fanged Mirrors were their actual owners, the mirrors could also be used as potent weapons.

History
On an unknown world, the Great Matriarch of the Sea of Black Sand, Yehcir-Eya, was reaching the end of her lifespan. Fearing for the stability of her nation, which was beset on all sides by rivals that wished to claim her lands, the Great Matriarch sought to ally herself with one of these Lesser Matriarchs, yet she did not know who to trust. She asked her oracles to devise a way to test the hearts of her potential allies, and they urged her to travel to the very edge of the Sea of Black Sand to find what she sought. After a long journey on foot, Yehcir-Eya discovered a courtyard-sized plate of silvered glass embedded in the desert floor. Her oracles instructed her to cut the glass plate into thirty-three smaller mirrors, which were then sent to each of the Lesser Matriarchs as a gift.

As it happened, every Lesser Matriarch who received a Fanged Mirror fell dead, with stories circulating about them being murdered by crawling spectral forms. Yehcir-Eya's murderous gift was considered a grave provocation, and the surrounding realms all invaded her nation and razed it to the ground. The Fanged Mirrors were scattered and forgotten.

One Fanged Mirror was eventually found by the rogue modron Nordom in Sigil.

Reputation
Exactly why the souls manifested by the Fanged Mirrors were so murderous was unclear. It was certainly possible that the plate of glass Yehcir-Eya discovered simply happened to be evil in and of itself. Another possibility was that the Lesser Matriarchs who died all had souls that were consumed by greed and dreams of conquest.

Notable Owners

## Faraneyth's Redemption

Faraneyth's Redemption was the unique enchanted belt that once belonged to Faraneyth, a cursed paladin who lived some years before the late 14 century DR.

Powers
The belt possessed a powerful protective +4 enchantment and granter the wearer magical regeneration and posted their will.

History
Faraneyth was a devotee paladin of Torm until one day, he was driven mad by a cursed set of armor, and drove him to murder. He was banished for the order in shame. Faraneyth took on a penance of a traveling preacher, praising his deity actors the Realms. One day, the disgraced paladin came across an evil cult that took over a temple. He encouraged locals to take up arms against valiantly stood against evil, being gravely injured in the process. As he laid dying, a giant eagle appeared to him, healing the wounds and returning Faraneyth divine Tormish powers. His belt, which was used to stop the bleeding, received a portion of benevolent godly powers, becoming Faraneyth's Redemption.

In the, Faraneyth's Redemption was found in the hands of Mother Feodorra, a charm and magic item peddler from the Rashemi city of Mulsantir.

## Fated Winds

Fated Winds was a trading cartel active in Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Organization
The Fated Winds coster was run by Jordan Wright circa 1374 DR. It was one of three major trade cartels granted offices within the Temple of Waukeen following the Shadow War in Neverwinter's Tyr's Eye district.

History
Before the, Fated Winds had an unsavory reputation as there were many rumors that claimed that the coster had dealings with the Zhentarim. These rumors persisted up until the advent of the Shadow War. When the conflict was over, Northwest Faerûn's trade suffered greatly. Lord Nasher Alagondar personally invited the Church of Waukeen to establish a temple in Neverwinter and aid the region's financial recovery. Fated Winds gained the trust of the church after they submitted the coster's ledgers for an investigation that came up clean, observing them from any implied wrongdoings through the Black Network. Nevertheless, some merchants and members of the church still mistrusted Jordan Wright and his coster.

## Fables of Faerûn

Fables of Faerûn was a collection of short stories and parables collected from across the Realms.

Description
Each entry in the Fables of Faerûn was a rather thin book with a green cover.

Content
Volume I: The Dimwitted Drow
The tale told of a drow wizard of Menzoberranzan named Zad, who studied the Art in Sorcere. There, the academy's Archmage met with Zad every tenday to see the wizard's progression in magic and every tenday Zad failed. The Archmage threatened Zad with expulsion due to his poor scholarship. Desperate to remain with Sorcere, the wizard snuck out at night into Oryndoll, the city of mind flayers, to peruse its massive arcane library. But even finding the city proved to be a failure. The longer Zad searched for it in the Underdark, the more lost he got. Eventually, the wizard collapsed, hungry and tired. When he regained consciousness, Zad woke up surrounded by books of Oryndoll, restrained. The next thing he saw was an illithid tadpole entering his left eye.

Volume II: The Daring Duergar
The Daring Duergar told the story of Shull, a young duergar psionicist who was eager to attain as much power as he could. Shull was directed by an individual known as the Godfather to attain greater power from the mind flayer Ir, who dwelled in a chasm near the Darklake.

Shull learned all of Ir's secrets, including the ability to destroy a creature's mind by thought alone. Ir warned Shull however, the power could only be used one before it became "forever forgotten". Shull returned to the Godfather, eager to demonstrate his new power. Shull grinned at his master and used the mind-destroying power, exploding his own skull in the process. The Godfather had completed Shull's final lesson.

Volume III: The Enticing Elder
The Enticing Elder was a story about two sisters, one a powerful warrior and another a devout cleric, and their journeys through the Underdark. During their four-tenday adventure, they slew all manner of monsters and creatures, including derro, darklings, bulettes, and bugbears. At the end of their trek, they came across a colony of mind flayers, and slew many of their numbers as well.

Finally, the sisters awakened the elder brain Qaoz in the heart of the mind flayer colony. Each stated their desires: the fighter sought to destroy the aberration and end its dominion over the region, while the cleric wanted to take on its psionic powers as her own. After a brief moment, the elder brain easily slew each sister with little more than a thought and fed their remains to the surviving mind flayers.

Volume V: The Boy and the Beholder
This story told of a small boy who lived with his shepherd father on the banks of the River Chionthar. Bored with his humdrum life, the boy repeatedly ran around yelling "Beholder! Beholder!", much to his father's alarm. The boy laughed in amusement time and again as he scared his father with this game.

A few days after he had tricked his father, the boy saw a large, spherical figure, with distinct eye stalks, floating off in the distance. The "beholder" stalked the boy along the river's edge until he could run no more. The creature revealed was in fact the shepherd himself, dressed in a cloak and wreathed in vines. The shepherd got his revenge on his son, and taught him a valuable lesson he would not forget.

Volume VI: The Cheerful Deep Gnome
The sixth volume of the series shared the tale of the indomitable deep gnome slave-miner named Doyle. Doyle and his brother worked tirelessly for their duergar overlords, and were pushed to the point of exhaustion, yet Doyle always kept a smile. He would even say he 'wished his masters much success', as they mined ore for duergar weaponry. As the duergar created more and better wares, they brought on more slaves to their operations, and Doyle became all the more elated.

Until the final day when there were 21 deep gnomes including Doyle and his brother. Doyle whooped with such glee that the duergar avoided his presence for peace and quiet some ways away. It was then that Doyle and the gnomes all equipped themselves with mithral armor and weaponry, and set upon their tormentors. Doyle remained ever-joyful throughout the entire rebellion.

Fank and Chock spent much of their debating what various humanoid creatures tasted like, along with whether or not they should be considered 'friends' or 'food'.

In the, Fank, Chock, and Lump were hired by the cult of the Absolute. The trio were stationed in the ruins of Moonhaven, directed to check passersby for anyone bearing the mark of the Absolute. ## Fael

Description
Fael was a human man with light skin and light chestnut hair. He wore a set of enchanted +1 leather armor and wielded a war hammer.

History
Fael traded in his humble stock of common potions and a small collection of books, including tomes about Halruaa, Zhentarim, waterdeep, and the Drow. In the, the village of Imnesvale faced a slew of disappearances, and locals did not hesitate to blame the legendary Umar Witch. Fael was worried by the disappearances but, even more so, was alerted by the disappearance of the local ranger protector Merella, who disappeared after venturing into the hills in search of the first victim of disappearances. However, in reality, he cared naught for local peasants and was working discreetly with Rejiek Hidesman, a serial killer, to create a heinous enchanted item on behalf of the Twisted Rune – a set of armor called the Human Flesh. It was, indeed, made out of human flesh.

## Favin Kreeger

Favin Kreeger  was an albino dwarf who resided in Port Nyanzaru's Old City as of the late 15 century DR. He made a living by hiding corpses in the city's refuse pit.

Description
Favin dressed in rags and would sweat profusely when he exerted himself. He was surprisingly nimble.

Personality
Favin was a compulsive liar and a nervous, sniveling coward. He was quick to whine in the face of discomfort and to grovel in the face of intimidation. He had no loyalties, even to family, and cared only for his own self-preservation and comfort. While he was a criminal, he was not a killer and lacked the courage to engage in fighting.

Relationships
He offered his body-hiding services to the criminals of Port Nyanzaru, and charged a hefty fee to make the corpses of murder victims disappear. He was also not above accepting odd jobs from suspicious individuals.

History
Shortly after the end of the death curse in the late 15 century DR, Favin crossed paths with an undead necromancer, Jaru Tasca, while hiding a Chultan man's body in the refuse pit. She offered him 50 gold pieces to deliver a letter to the beggar prince Pock-Marked Po, and threatened to kill him if he revealed anything about her identity. He accepted the job in part out of fear, and unbeknownst to him, the letter was intended to push Po to send adventurers into a trap.

Upon delivering the letter, Po was concerned that neither he nor his contacts recognized the letter's handwriting, and when Favin refused to divulge who had given him the letter, Po had the dwarf thrown into Executioner's Run to scare him into speaking. This worked, and Favin revealed what he knew—including the location of the secret tunnel from which he had seen Jaru emerge—and hoped that adventurers would slay the necromancer to ensure his safety.

## Famine hound

Famine hounds were a variety of undead dog that existed in the period between the Spellplague and the Second Sundering. They arose from the bodies of dogs that had been abandoned and left to die of starvation.

Description
Famine hounds had gaunt, hollow bodies.

Ability
Like most undead, they were resistant to harm from necrotic energy and vulnerable to radiant energy.

History
Around the late 15 century DR, a human necromancer from the Bleak Academy was operating in Tymanther, in an ancient Untheri burial site near Ruinspoke, creating a number of undead deep within a mausoleum.It is not specified in the adventure whether the ruins being explored belonged to Unther or Tymanchebar. However, due to the adventure only making note of ruins of "the old Untheric Empire" and the presence of a Lathanderite sarcophagus in the mausoleum, it stands to reason the ruins were from Unther. Three of these undead were famine hounds, which fought to protect the necromancer when a group of adventurers invaded his lair to rescue the kidnapped dwarf Melnar.

Ecology
Like many undead dogs, famine hounds were drawn towards cemeteries and other places of death. They sometimes raided towns, though preferred to live on the fringes and pick off hapless travelers. Having died of starvation, these undead had an endless hunger for the flesh of the living.

In the, Manip Farthingale oversaw the arrest and conviction of criminals from the Stonyeyes Precinct of the Flaming Fist. During The Absolute Crisis, Farthingale handled two prisoners of note: Durm Goroy and Vince Torell. Torell was accused of being a spy for the Absolute and treason, while Goroy was arrested after being accused of an assassination attempt on Lord Enver Gortash. ## Farloi

Farloi was a bold tasloi fighter and one of the two lieutenants of the Flotilla of DEATH.

Description
Like other tasloi, Farloi had bright green skin and black eyes, but he lacked any hair. He was taller than his fellow tasloi, standing . He sported a muscular build and weighed around .

Personality
Farloi was quite a bold individual. He often sought to prove his bravery and valor by undertaking unusual risks. However, he was also quite harsh overseer of his fellow crewmen, always insisting they act more civilized and pushing them to act bravely like him. He took particular umbrage from his fellow tasloi dragging their knuckles on the ground when they walked.

Possessions
Farloi typically dressed himself in a white sailor's uniform adorned with three copper medals that no particular worth or significance. Like the other tasloi on the Flotilla of DEATH, he was typically armed with a javelin, though he also wielded a dagger. Notable among his possessions was a ring of jumping, which he would use to jump from the back of giant gooney birds and onto his opponents.

History
Farloi was born sometime around the, on the island of Tasloiomitasloi,Port of Ravens Bluff (pp. 42, 45) states that Charles O'Kane became Mayor 20 years ago, which would set it in 1361 DR. Working backwards from this, Faroi's birth year can be extrapolated. as the third cousin of the island's high chieftain. Sometime later Spitvah McGurk discovered the island and struck up a bargain with the native tasloi to form a small seafaring fleet called the Flotilla of DEATH, wherein Farloi would be promoted to the role of lieutenant alongside Gisloi. Farloi in particular enlisted most of his clansmen, as he desired to prove their combat prowess.

## Face Crag

Defenses
Decades prior to, a merchant had placed several braziers around Face Crag. When lit at dark, they concealed those camping on the eastern side of the cleft from sight, making the area more defensible.

After the first murder in Orthil Voldovan's caravan in late, he sought to make it to Face Crag, expecting attack from the locals in the Blackrocks otherwise. He made it there before nightfall.

Belgon Bradaskor, when asked by Sharantyr how to find Orthil Voldovan's caravan, was directed there. She arrived there soon, but to do so, she had to ride the horse Flamewind too severely. Nonetheless, the horse accompanied her there.

## Fall-from-Grace

Fall-from-Grace was a succubus cleric and philosopher, who conquered her evil roots and after escaping the lower planes became the proprietress of the Brothel for Slaking Intellectual Lusts in the Clerks' Ward district of Sigil.

Description
Fall-from-Grace was a strikingly beautiful woman. She had long-golden hair, azure eyes and two sleek wings. She frequently wore a dark blue and violet tunic.

She could not stand the feeling of the touch of metal like many of her kin, and thus would never carry any such objects. Thus, if a need for combat had arisen, she would use her lethal succubus kiss as a weapon.

Personality
Fall-from-Grace in many ways was the polar opposite of the succubus archetype. She would not rely on her beautiful appearance to persuade anybody. Additionally, she was undeniably chaste and had no interest in pleasures of the flesh. She valued a good conversation and new intellectual experiences above all, to the point where she founded an establishment dedicated to just that.

She was extremely perceptive, skilled in the art of discussion and exceedingly intelligent (something which allowed her to escape the Nine Hells). She was very calm and composed, able to retain a clear mind in all conditions. She was very cordial towards everybody at all times, even if the other party was downright insulting her.

Possessions
Fall-from-Grace wielded an enchanted hornblade despite relying on her clerical and succubus powers in battle. She always wore her ring of mind shielding and never parted with a pair of green crystal eyes of seeing.

Relationships
She was a daughter of Red Shroud, the succubus ruler of the Broken Reach fortress in Pazunia. Due to her lineage, Fall-from-Grace faced a lot of prejudice from other people. It was difficult for her to earn the trust of anybody, as most presumed she had ulterior motives. For instance, Vrischika the Importer, the alu-fiend proprietor of the Curiosity Shoppe located not far from Grace's establishment, was stubborn in saying that her behavior and good-aligned values were a part of a greater ruse. Nonetheless, the workers and patrons of her brothel have grown to trust her and appreciate her in full.

History
When Fall-from-Grace was still very young, she was sold into baatezu slavery by her own mother, despite the succubi being allied with the tanar'ri at the time, and thus being the devils' sworn enemies. She spent many long years enduring mental torture in the Nine Hells. During this time, she took the opportunity to hone her conversational and intellectual skills, and eventually through clever interpretation of language and infernal contracts, she was able to break her captivity and escape.

Sometime after she was freed, she ended up within Sigil, where she joined the Society of Sensation, which allowed her to to further pursue the experiences and development that she sought. Not far from the headquarters of the Sensates, she established the Brothel for Slaking Intellectual Lusts, which allowed its customers to engage in intellectual debate and conversation with its workers.

## Faereld's Hold

Faereld's Hold was a keep in the western part of Sembia.

Location
The country manor had been built on a hill covered with woods to the north of the River Arkhen and just a short distance east of the boundary between Archendale and Sembia. The hold was encircled by thick, dim woods, and only a few individuals chose to live there.

Structure
Faereld's Hold was built of stone. During its history, the original keep was expanded with new wings. Although these annexes to the building had worn out and broken by the late 15 century DR, the main building still stood fast.

The keep included some dungeons. At some point, monsters from the Underdark tunneled into these dungeons.

## Fang Island

Fang Island was a small island that sat in the mouth of the River Mirar in the city of Luskan.

Description
The island was uninhabited and craggy, and earned its name because its sharp rocks were known to tear apart any boat or barge that was swept downriver. It hosted a self-sustaining area of wild magic that would randomly discharge dangerous spells. Although rare, these magical bursts could illuminate a dark night in the city.

Geography
The island lay between Harbor Arm Island and the inhabited islands in the River Mirar's mouth (Blood Island, Closeguard Island, and Cutlass Island).

History
The Arcane Brotherhood had plans to build on the barren Fang Island as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR, and its wild magic zone was the result of a failed effort by them to create an extremely powerful ward there. They subsequently were unable to remove this phenomenon.

In the, the island was seized by Deudermont and Ship Kurth, and was used as part of a blockade of the river in their battle to drive Arklem Greeth and the Arcane Brotherhood out of the city.

During the traumatic events of the Spellplague in the, the island was suffused with fire and light as it lifted into the air as an earthmote. It remained above the city for a century, occasionally spawning terrifying monsters or spilling acid rain.

Following the Second Sundering, it had returned to the river and was once again noted for its tendency to tear ships apart. It remained uninhabited.

## Far Horns Forest

The Far Horns Forest was a massive forest that covered much of north Faerûn during the time of Netheril. It once made up much of the western and southern frontier of Netheril, but over time was much reduced in extent until it was broken up into smaller remnant forests.

Description
The Far Horns were named for the region's ability to carry sounds long distances. It was possible for a man standing on the Greypeak Mountains to blow a horn and have it heard as far away as the Flats, while other noises from the forest floor could be carried many miles away as the local twisted air currents aided in their transmission.

Geography
The Far Horns once stretched between the High Forest and the expansive plains of Netheril, reaching from the foothills of the Barren Mountains in the north almost to the Bodensee River and the Hollow in the south. In the north, it bordered on the Rengath ancestral lands, while towards the south it met the Greypeak Mountains, the Purple Mountains, the Flats, and the Moss, as well as the Angardt ancestral lands.

The forest surrounded much of the upper Winding Water as it flowed out of the Moss, and also served as the origin for the Sunderiver and Moray River, two tributaries of the Zweihaus River.

Inhabitants
The Thundercall orcs lived in the northern parts of the forest and were infamous for their fearsome warhorns, but were kept from being a threat by aggressive patrols from Unity. Another tribe, the Greenhaven orcs, lived in the central reaches of the forest near the Moss. They raided the nearby Netherese settlements and used flaming arrows to hunt the trolls in the Moss, until they were destroyed by the vengeful trolls. Those trolls in turn used the Far Horns as their hunting ground.

Many gnomes who had escaped from the slavery of Netheril ended up settling in the Far Horns, with forest gnomes consolidating in the later Forgotten Forest. Wood giants, satyrs, and nymphs also lived within the forest.

History

The city of Unity was founded within the edge of the forest in, becoming a military base for fighting against local goblins and other threats.

 saw the founding of Westwendt Village near the Mines of Dekanter, serving as a central market with the elves of Illefarn and Eaerlann, and the dwarves of Delzoun.

During the Silver Age of Netheril ( to ), the Greenhaven orcs were wiped out by the furious trolls they had hunted.

During the Golden Age of Netheril ( to ), orcs, goblins, and kobolds began aggressively attacking Westwendt, leading to an extended period of war for the town and the surrounding area.

The city of Wreathe was founded in  at the foot of the Purple Mountains, and became a frontier mining town famed for the hunting opportunities in the nearby wilderness.

A beholder spelljammer crash-landed near Westwendt in, causing a large fire that destroyed much of the local forest.

Wreathe was attacked in  by three ancient blue dragons, the children of Brightstrike. Seeking revenge for their mother's slaying hundreds of years earlier, the wyrms slowly destroyed the city over a period of days.

The bandit lord Olostin sited himself in Dread Keep and began raiding western Netheril in, starting with a gruesome attack on Unity. He was finally slain in, and was discovered to have been working with the phaerimm.

The archwizard Karsus established his enclave in, and proceeded to hover over the Far Horns during the winter months, much to the displeasure of the locals.

With the spread of the Anauroch desert caused by the phaerimms after, the folk of Low Netheril migrated away in search of more liveable lands, with many moving into the Far Forest. Within a few centuries the combination of deforestation and desertification had severely reduced the forest and fragmented it into the Forgotten Forest, Northdark Woods, Forest of Wyrms, Weathercote Wood, and Far Forest, with the empty ground between them becoming the Fallen Lands, the Lonely Moor, and Pelleor's Prairie. These Netherese settlers pressed on the traditional lands of Najara and even colonized the eastern Serpent Hills, forcing the native ophidians and lizardfolk into retreat.

A horde of fiends attacked Unity in, leading to a three-year war that had Karsus himself leading a counter-attack that banished or slew the extra-planar invaders. In the same year, fiends attacked Westwendt and razed it to the ground. The use of heavy magic during the conflict by Karsus's forces led to magically-mutated monsters and other oddities emerging from the forest in .

The fall of Netheril in  saw the exodus of the entire surviving Netherese population, many of them migrating into the Backlands region and forcing the remaining ophidians to abandon the Serpent Hills entirely and take refuge in nearby Ss'thar'tiss'ssun while they regained their strength. The city of Unity survived the Fall, but was slowly depopulated and abandoned as the Anauroch encroached on it over time.

Notable Locations
 Aumvor's castle, the home of Aumvor the Undying.
 Dread Keep, the fortress of the bandit lord Olostin.
 Eileanar, the flying enclave of Karsus, spent its winter months hovering over the forest near the Winding Water, and annoyed the locals enough to name the river the "Karsus Overwash."
 The Hill of Lost Souls, a dormant volcano.
 Saharelgard, the home of the archmage Saharel.
 Unity, a Netherese city on the edge of the northern forest dedicated to fighting goblins and orcs.
 Westwendt Village, a Netherese settlement that served as a market town for elves and dwarves.
 Wreathe, a Netherese city at the base of the Purple Mountains.

## False Prophecy

False Prophecy is a one round adventure module set in the Living City organized play program. It was published in July 1993 and is intended for adventurers of any level. The module received a reprint as the Adventure I of the Fall Season 1998 Adventurer's Guild.

Synopsis
A group of adventurers of the Living City found themselves amidst a conflict between the Church of Waukeen, who constructed a Shrine to Waukeen in the city's Harbor District, and the devote worshipers of Selûne, who believed the harbor to be their church's territory. The Selûnites concocted a scheme to defraud the shrine's money and undermine its stability with the help of Lar & Sons Imports, who supplied the construction with crumbly bricks. Adventurers followed a trail of clues and a false Selûnite prophesy when the shrine collapsed. Subsequently, they uncovered the corruption and helped Lady Lauren DeVillars to apprehend the perpetrators.

## Fastidious Fungi

Fastidious Fungi was a book penned by J. L. Corrins and published sometime in or before the late 15 century DR.

Content
Fastidious Fungi included some information on hags, presumably the pearlspore bell mushrooms that could be used to return a slain hag to the land of the living. However, that lore was thought to be pure speculation by some.

History
In the, a copy of Fastidious Fungi was studied by Mayrina after forming the Anti-Hag Support Group, in search of ways to stop hags' sinister and malicious plots.

Appearances

## Fairy dust

Fairy dust was a naturally-occurring substance that possessed magical properties.

Description
Fairy dust was a life byproduct of various fey creatures like sprites. It was dust made by the fey from their own shed hair and skin. The fine substance could be identified by its soft golden radiance in the darkness, yet it was almost invisible, and it sparkled in normal light. In moonlight, the dust shimmered with a silvery sheen.

Powers
Fairy dust was given to their friends and allies as a gift by the fey. It had many well-documented uses in magic, including boosting the power of spells. An ounce of fairy dust could be added as an additional material component when casting spells of the illusion school to boost their effectiveness. This led to some of the fairy species being hunted to the brink of extinction in Thay. Transmutation spells received a similar boost from an ounce of the substance – it doubled the spells' duration.

Rumors & Legends
The origin of fairy dust had been a subject of scholarly debate. Some scholars believed that it was made from the first spring blossoming of forest flowers, while others argued that it was powdered cocoons of a rare moth species that bred in the spring.

## Facemaker's Boutique

Facemaker's Boutique, also known as Facemaker Fashion, was a busy upscale clothing shop located in the Bloomridge neighborhood of the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

Location
Facemaker's Boutique was located on Wendserpent Street, up the street south from The Glitter Gala, just by the Manor Gate of the Old Wall, which connected the Lower and Upper Cities.

Interior
The Boutique's entrance was marked with a sign shaped like two thread spools and advertised the establishment's name. Past the main entrance stood the shop's main room with a clerk's counter in the middle, two large window displays of finery and shelves and racks that showed the shop's wide selection of clothes, dyes, gowns, cloaks, boots, and hats.

The clerk's counter had two large doors on each side that opened into the Facemaker's workshop, which consisted of three interconnected rooms: the clothier to the left and mirror-filled hair-styling to the right, and a lavish waiting area in the middle, separated from the two other rooms by red curtains.

The Boutique's second floor was used as living quarters.

Services
Facemaker's Boutique offers a wide variety of fanciful clothes for purchase, styling, and custom-tailoring. Additionally, the establishment helped its customers with makeup and hairstyling, as well as other cosmetic services.

History
Facemaker's Boutique once was owned by Julio Facemaker, sometime before the late 15 century DR. After Julio passed, his daughter Carmen Facemaker received a massive inheritance of gold, gems, and silks, while the shop and all the hard work and responsibilities were left to his son, Figaro "Facemaker" Pennygood, because, as the last will and testament stated, "A bit of honest labor would do him good." However, there were some doubts about the last will's authenticity due to the presence of a forgery kit near where the original document was hidden in the Boutique.

In the, during the Absolute crisis, Figaro Pennygood was targeted by the Bhaalist Unholy Assassin aspirant Dolor, who went on a spree of grizzly murders collecting lives and hands of the innocent of Baldur's Gate. Figaro was on the list of targets, and subsequently, an attempt on his life took place. Dolor, accompanied by a group of Bhaalist doppelgangers, poisoned the dwarf clothier with Karabasan's gift and set out to slice his neck open. Devella Fountainhead, a constable of the Flaming Fist, interfered, being attracted by the assault's commotion, but was rendered paralyzed by the same toxin. It was unknown whether Figaro survived the attempt on his life or was slaughtered by the Murder Tribunal.

During the same period of time, Figaro Pennygood posted an ad searching for a cut hair sweeper who had to come with their own broom.

Inhabitants
Phlanders Hort was one of the clerks employed by Master Figaro in the late 15 century DR, often found at the center of the shop, behind the counter. Another one of his employees was Bobula Hanvers. The shop also was supplied with gems by Omotola and her Glitter Gala shop nearby.

Some of the Facemaker's Boutique's customers included Hannaah Befling; Varce Humbletood, who used the Facemaker's hair cutting and styling services; the vampire spawn Leon, who served Cazador Szarr; Master Durinbold, who commissioned formalwear for his wife to attend Lord Enver Gortash's coronation; as well as an anonymous client who sought to change his appearance to avoid assassination.

## Fanatic retaliation

Fanatic retaliation was a unique evocation spell from the third circle of magic used in the late 15 century DR.

Effects
The fanatic retaliation spell was cast as a reaction, mere moments after the caster's ally was slain. The spell always targeted the creature that landed the killing blow by launching a powerful psionic barrage that had a chance of rendering the target silenced for twelve seconds. Creatures of formidable intelligence had a chance of reduce the damage by half and shrug the magical silence off. This magical rebuke had the range of . The effects of fanatic retaliation could not be strengthened by a casting at a higher proficiency level.

History
This unique sell was known and was used by the drider Kar'niss, a worshiper of the Absolute in the . He would unleash the deadly spell when one of his spindleweb fanatics was slain.

Appearances

## Falmor Duguir

Falmor Duguir was a reader of the Baldur's Mouth Gazette broadsheet from the Lower City of Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

History
In the, Falmor Duguir came across an issue of Baldur's Mouth Gazette on his recently passed uncle's desk. The issue's puzzle section titled "Win a Peek at Candlekeep" was half finished. Falmor put some time and effort into the puzzle and eventually completed it. He penned a letter for the Puzzle Editor of the Gazette and enclosed the solved puzzle, asking who he would need to talk to claim the prize. Falmor also asked if he were to come to the Baldur's Mouth building in the Lower City, would Ettvard Needle be the person to talk to, as he also had a piece of his mind to give to the Editor-in-Chief.

Soon after, a gang assembled by Churg Elvek, secretly a cultist of the Absolute, sought to strengthen the cult's grasp on Baldur's Gate. When the famed writer Volothamp Geddarm returned to the Gate after misadventures in the Emerald Grove and coming face-to-face with the Absolute's army, Volo became a target for Churg and his gang. The master traveler was captured and tied to a stack of smokepowder barrels, ready to be "brought to justice" for being an alleged agent of the Absolute at the hands of the gang and street gawkers. Falmor Duguir was one of the commoners in the gathered crowd, who was swayed by Churg Elvek's accusations. Luckily, the band known as the Absolute Adversaries interrupted the street execution.
