## Fang (disambiguation)

Fang could refer to several landmarks:
 The Fang, a peninsula in Aglarond,
 The Fangs, a pass in the Cloud Peaks,
 Fang (Laerakond), a mountain in Laerakond,
 Fang Rocks, a reef in the Pirate Isles,
 Fang of Skulls, a landmark in Dead Orc Pass,
 Fang Island, an island in Luskan, or
 Fang in the Forest, a peak in the Earthfast Mountains.

It could also refer to certain items:
 Fang (weapon), a Kara-Turan weapon,
 Fang (sword), a magical shortsword from Chult,
 Fang dagger, a small venomous blade,
 Fanged mask, a mask that gave the wearer fangs,
 Fanged Mirror of Yehcir-Eya, a set of dangerous mirrors,
 Fang of Zehir, a yuan-ti blade, or
 Fang scarab, a gift of Lolth.

Many beings used the name Fang:
 Fang (dog), a fierce fenhound,
 Fang dragon, a gray chromatic dragon,
 Fangs of Night, a ninja group in Koryo,
 Fangs of Set, a secret cabal in Mulhorand,
 Fang of Talona, the priests of Talona,
 Fang of Yeenoghu, the gnoll champions of Yeenoghu, or
 Fangshields, a group of heroic animals.

Some spells also used the name:
 Fang trap, which set a biting trap,
 Fang fist, which gave the caster's hand a biting mouth, or
 Fangs of retribution, a spell that inflicted vengeful wounds,

Published adventures also used the term:
 Fang and Claw, an adventure set in Avernus, or
 Fangs and Frogs, an adventure set in Waterdeep.

## Fair Maid

The Fair Maid was a tavern in Ripplestones.

Structure
Outside, there was at least one table set down where it didn't get too much direct sunlight during the morning, on a corner of the porch.

Interior
There were tables inside.

Services
Food and drink were served at the tavern. As of, local dishes included soup served in tureens, redruck cheese, and grouse pies during Elminster's visit. A later serving included steaming turtles and buttered snails. According to a repeat customer, a cook from a long time before, "Old Raider", was good at roasting buck.

Stable boys were available to mind horses.

History
The tavern was visited by Elminster in the mid–8 century; there, he had Caladaster and Baerdagh over for a meal in exchange for information on Scorchstone Hall. Ilbryn Starym turned up asking about him a few days later, but did not buy anything; they warned him that Elminster had since headed for the Dead Place.

Another few days later, Tabarast, Beldrune, and an unnamed Harper turned up, also asking about Elminster; Caladaster Daermree joined them, and warned them about the Slayer, something that haunted the Dead Place. Baerdagh was also revealed to be the Lion of Elversult, an old hero, and was goaded into talking about his exploits.

Later, while Tabarast, Beldrune, and Caladaster were telling their tale of meeting Azuth at Karsus' Keep, the undead sorceress Sharindala entered the tavern. She asked for the dress robe that Tabarast had received from Azuth as a reward; as he refused, Caladaster gave his own clothes over to her, and she took him over to her home to discuss something.

## Faeya

Faeya was an apprentice wizard in the mid–8 century DR.

Description
She was lushly built. During, she wore at least once a gown of forest green. Her eyes were lively brown. According to Thessamel Arunder, her belly was flat and hard.

Abilities
She was quite handy with a dagger, and with remedies. Her master thought her capable of casting spells.

Activities
Faeya apprenticed under Thessamel Arunder.

Relationships
As well as his student, she was Thessamel Arunder's lover.

History
During the Chaos of the Art in the, Faeya had to kill two men who sought revenge against her master. She then sought out a number of merchants who had offered to buy the lands of Thessamel Arunder and gotten been earlier, then convinced them to repeat their offer. She then got Thessamel Arunder, her master, to sign their offer during a moment of play in bed. She explained the situation to him over the next morning, in a meeting with the aforementioned merchants.

## Farlin

Farlin was a member of The Guild crime gang in Baldur's Gate and an Envoy of Nine-Fingers to the Stone Lord in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Farlin was a rock gnome woman with a pale skin tone and green eyes. She wore a leather helmet and leather armor dyed in emerald-green—the Guild's telltale color. She was armed with two daggers: a mundane one, and an enchanted Gleamdance dagger.

History
In the, during the Absolute crisis, Farlin led a gang of Guild members in dealing with the dreaded Stone Lord and his men. The new kingpin secretly served the Absolute and was trying to remove the Guild's influence from across the Gate. Farlin's group faced a band of the Stone Lord's men led by the tiefling Cairos, under the Wyrm's Crossing bridge in Rivington, hoping to seize their illegal goods haul. However, the meeting devolved into a battle. Farlin's and her squad's fate remained uncertain, whether they were killed by the gangsters or survived and returned safely to the Guild's safehouse.

## Falataer

Falataer was a powerful bard, musician, and instrument maker from the Moonshae Isles, famed for creating the original Instruments of the Bards of Toril.

History
Long before the 14 century DR, Falataer was known as a wise teacher and a bard of impressive powers. He ran a bardic college on the Moonshae Isles and tested his students with the Instruments of the Bards, later gifting them to his best students. Over the centuries, Falataer's designs were copied, and the instrument retained the great bard's name as a sign of respect. Many of the copies Instruments of the Bards were less powerful than his originals, however.

By the, it was believed that there were only about twenty Falataeric Cli lyres left in existence, at least those exquisite lyres that were hand-crafted by the great bard in his workshop back on the Moonshae Isles. One of these instruments was sold at the Chromatic Scale, a musical instrument shop in the Lower City of Baldur's Gate.

## Far hand

Far hand was a basic psionic power of the psychokinesis discipline that enabled a minor form of telekinesis.

Effects
For a period of up to a minute of concentration, this power allowed a psion to mentally lift, move, and propel mundane objects, weighing around, anywhere within . This power would cease if the distance between the psion and object ever exceeded this range. Through exerting themselves mentally, psions were capable of expanding this power's range in increments of  and the power's weight limit in increments of .

Display
Manifesting this power produced a standard visual display.

Users
Far hand was found to be an innate psionic ability for blue goblins, githyanki, and tsucora quori.

Known/Notable Users

 Saryndalaghlothtor, an adult crystal dragon.
 Selinah Varhast, a human psion and assassin.
 Zelia, a yuan-ti telepath.

## Fador

Fador was a worker of the Lake of Steam region in the late 14 century DR.

History
He worked at the canal site that was being constructed by the city-state of Innarlith to connect the Sea of Fallen Stars to the Lake of Steam in the .

He and some other workers noticed that Ivar Devorast had clandestinely returned to the canal site. Devorast had previously headed the project before he was dismissed by the city's ruler, Ransar Salatis. Fador approached Devorast and promised to keep his secret, but asked for his advice concerning orders by Senator Horemkensi to use a smaller amount of sand than Devorast had originally instructed. Devorast insisted that they return to his original design and Fador agreed to convey this to the others. In order to avoid Horemkensi from becoming suspicious that the work wasn't speeding up, Fador also followed a suggestion from Devorast's friend Surero to lie to Horemkensi about slowing down the work to reshod the horses.

## Falcon (ship)

Falcon was the name of the flagship of the Golden Legion, an Amnish mercenary company that invaded Maztica in 1361 DR.

History
Captain-General Cordell and his Golden Legion sailed westwards from Amn in pursuit of a new route to Kara-Tur. Their expedition included several ships, with the Falcon serving as Cordell's flagship. The ship sailed with the fleet from Amn through Asavir's Channel and stopped in Lantan to load up on provisions before sailing westwards. It continued its westward journey until it arrived at the Green Sisters archipelago in Maztica. The fleet then continued southwards until it reached Ulatos, where it disembarked and the legion got into a conflict with the local Payit people that ended with the legion conquering their city, plundering their gold and slaughtering many of their people.

Captain Halloran, one of Cordell's officers, was briefly held in the brig of the Falcon by Cordell shortly before the battle with the Payit, after Domincus accused him of being responsible for his daughter's death at the hands of the Payit. Domincus sent Captain Alvarro to secretly kill Halloran in the brig while the legion was disembarked on shore for the battle. However, Halloran overcame Alvarro, escaped his confinement and looted some of the possessions of the legion's mage Darien from her quarters on the Falcon before escaping to shore.

An invisible stalker attempted to kill Darien as she slept with Cordell in his quarters after she had sent the same stalker to kill Halloran following his escape. She defeated the stalker, but the event made her realize that it had failed in its mission to kill Halloran.

Following the great plundering of gold in Ulatos, Cordell wished to move further inland to plunder Nexal when he learned of the wealth of this city. However, the assessor Kardann, who had been appointed by Amn's Council of Six to represent their authority on the expedition, urged that they should return to Amn with the treasure and get more reinforcements before they continued further inland. Cordell, when he realized that some of his captains sympathized with Kardann's view, had all of his ships, including Falcon, burned in the lagoon near Ulatos in sight of Kardann and the legion so that they understood that there was no going back.

Notable Passengers
 Captain-General Cordell
 Domincus and his daughter Martine
 Darien
 Kardann

In the, as the Absolute crisis ravaged the Western Heartlands, the city of Baldur's Gate became flooded with refugees. Mama Falice and her family were lucky enough to secure lodgings at Nortale's Hostel, which offered beds to the refugees but had very limited capacity. As the conflict continued to ravage the Chionthar Valley, Falice lamented the events that led her family into Baldur's Gate and often missed fresh air, being cooped up in a crowded hostel.

## Fang golem

Fang golems were a bestial form of golems composed of claws, fangs, tusks, and the like from various creatures.

Description
These golems were always designed to resemble some form of wild beast, which made them very diverse in both shape and size. For example, one could be constructed to resemble a giant crab, incorporating pincers and shark teeth into their makeup. The size of a fang golem was comparable to that of a dire animal, usually measuring  in length, and weighing upwards of .

Behavior
These golems were devoid of sentience, mindlessly following whatever orders were issued to them. They were incapable of coming up with their strategies or tactics, only following to the letter the instructions given to them. Due to their mindless nature, it was impossible to intimidate or provoke them, though if attacked they would respond in kind.

Fang golems were not solely devoted to their creators, but rather were capable of being commanded to obey the orders of another and that person in turn could command the golem to follow the commands of another. Additionally, they could even be commanded to govern their own actions in their master's absence. The creator of a fang golem could resume their absolute control over it at anytime by simplying commanding the construct to follow solely their orders.

When a fang golem went some time without being commanded, it would relentlessly following whatever last instruction was issued to it, to the best of its ability.

Abilities
Fang golems were impervious to most forms of energy-based attacks or spells and resistant to cold-based ones, but could be harmed by sonic-based ones, such as shout, greater shout, and orb of sound. They could see in areas of low-light as well as total darkness.

These golems could only receive orders if their masters were both audible and visible within  of them.

Fang golems could fire a barrage of spikes from their bodies, out to, up to five times per day. Additionally, any creature a fang golem harmed would become more susceptible to druid spells and fey abilities for a period of one minute.

A fang golem's body exploded upon being slain, sending the animal parts they were composed of out to  and potentially causing significant harm to anyone caught within the blast radius.

Ecology
These golems were typically created by either evil druids, to terrorize local pockets of civilization or guard their unholy druidic grove, or occasionally by powerful fey. Depending upon their shape, a fang golem could also act as a mount in noncombat situations. Non-evil druids considered this type of golem abominable and typically saw fit to destroy them, as well as their creators.
Habitats
Fang golems could be found in practically any form of environment that a creator saw fit, including underwater or hostile areas like volcanic caverns. However, because they were typically created by evil druids they were usually found in the forbidding places befitting them, such as corrupted forests, haunted wastelands, and underground tunnels.
Creation
Assembling a fang golem required  worths' of various fangs, claws, tusks, and the like. These animal parts would then be bound by cords or strips of animal hide to wood laths. These laths all had to be treated with preservative oils, costing around 500 gold pieces. Assembling these collections of animal parts could require killing a great deal of creatures, though this was not a requirement and the parts could be obtained by any means. Once the body of a fang golem was assembled a spirit had to be bound into its form. This final step of the creation process called for casting the spells greater magic fang, reincarnate, and spike stones.

The total cost of assembling a fang golem was at a minimum 8,000 gold pieces, whilst acquiring one or paying someone else for its creation could cost 15,000 gp.

History
In the late 14 century DR, when the Rotting Man terrorized the Rawlinswood in the Great Dale area, his minions constructed a number of fang golems from their stronghold of Dun-Tharos. They often acquired the necessary components for their construction from the remains of wildlife that were killed by corrupted ones' advance.

## Fatted Bookworm

The Fatted Bookworm was a business in the Lower Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
The Fatted Bookworm was a two-story building in deteriorating condition.

Services
The Fatted Bookworm served as a unique purveyor of rare books, scrolls, and high-quality writing materials within Skullport. The shop was renowned for its collection of tomes, parchment, vellum, and expertly bound volumes, all offered at premium prices.

Inhabitants
The Fatted Bookworm was owned by Velara Lillianyth, a human mage. Velara’s reputation ensured steady business, regardless of the presence of her occasional shop clerk, Amryyr Yauntyrr. Amryyr, a drow wizard and thief, utilized the shop as a base when in Skullport, dealing in rare texts, trading valuable information, and facilitating discreet item transfers between Skullport and other parts of the Underdark. Velara, who outwardly expressed little interest in Amryyr’s activities, allowed him to operate within her shop due to an obligation to his associate, Malakuth Tabuirr, a drow slaver. When Amryyr was present, the Fatted Bookworm occasionally handled highly coveted items, including rare magical texts, arcane scrolls, and, on rare occasions, spellbooks.

## False ioun stone

False ioun stone was a rare arcane spell from the alteration and illusion schools of magic, used by wizardly charlatans on the world of Oerth to create shallow copies of ioun stones.

Effects
False ioun stone was a spell from the first circle of magic that could create an illusion with a mere touch of the spellcaster. The mage could transform normal stones and pebbles into perfect copies of ioun stones they have had encountered in the past. The illusion created copies that looked and behaved identical to ioun stones but possessed no enchantments, floating around a person's head, and could be touched, moved, or repositioned without the spell being ruined and ruse discovered. These fakes also radiated appropriate magics, making the charade complete. The spell could create between one and three copies, depending on the number of material components used. If the spell was cast on a pebble by a mage who had never seen a real ioun stone, it produced a vibrant purple prism or a dusty rosy prism as the first item, a pearly white spindle or a lavender-and-green ellipsoid as the second, and a random stone as the third.

The illusion persisted for one minute, and its duration increased with the spellcaster's magical prowess.

Components
False ioun stone required verbal, somatic, and material components to be cast. Its material components were stones that served as the spell's targets, a handful of worthless glass beads, and a transparent gem, one per number of false ioun stones created. Once the spell was over, all components were consumed.

History
The false ioun stone spell was known to Rautheene of Greyhawk and it was among the spells exchanged between the members of The Wizards Three during their visit to Earth and raiding larders of Ed Greenwood of Canada. The spell was brought over to the world of Toril by Elminster of Shadowdale, a members of the same de facto group.

## Faline Abbot

Faline Abbot was a knight of the Purple Dragons of Cormyr, who was stationed at Wheloon Prison in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Faline was a human woman with light skin, ashy brown hair, and bright blue eyes. She wore her shoulder-length hair loose with some braids.

History

In the mid-1480s DR, Faline was posted at the docks of Wheloon Prison as part of the guard detail. When the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon began, the Cormyreans were kept unaware, though Faline Abbot noticed the unusual gang activity among the inmates. She was approached by the Amaunatori Dai Rolno, who impressed upon her the innocence of those trapped inside. When it reached her that the Wheloon Natives were being attacked by other gangs, she sent a group of hirelings into the prison to check on them and keep them safe, meeting Seth Wentalle at Harvest Hall. They then went into the Wheloon drainage culverts and fought off the gang led by Porto, Athea, and Aramiya Troise, who had sought to destroy the natives. With evidence of what was going on inside the prison, Faline was even more convinced that the situation was untenable and unjust, even if her superior Constal Davny Selkin didn't quite believe it.

## Faraway (illusion)

Faraway was a fictional village created by the demon lord Graz'zt as an elaborate magical trap to trick and destroy his enemies during the Rage of Demons in the late 15 century DR.

Description

The village was a mix of illusions and conjurations in the underground cavern, creating the realistic experience of being in a quaint rural village. The villagers were actually demons playing roles, mostly dretches and succubi. Adjacent to the village was Duke Bowen's castle, which had an elaborate hedge maze.

Geography
Faraway was narratively placed east of Daggerford on the Sword Coast, though it was truly somewhere in the Eastern Reaches of Tethyamar.

Government
In the village's story, the ruler was Duke Bowen, an elderly man with a few retainers who lived in an adjacent keep. The village was run by Mayor Thomas.

History

Graz'zt created the Faraway illusion during his schemes in Tethyamar, when he used the jewels of ardor to entrance and destroy both dwarves and drow. Once the war between them reached its climax, he disappeared with his Hal Stoutheart persona into the Eastern Reaches, prompting his assistant Inger Ingersdottir to send hirelings to find him. Instead, they unwittingly stepped from an Underdark cavern into the false village of Faraway. They were met by cheering crowds of locals heralding them as heroes, begging for to save the Duke from a drow invader.

At this point, the mercenary agents Zoraxzil the mind flayer and Silent the drow had been investigating Graz'zt's schemes on behalf of their employer, only to be trapped in the strange fake village and surrounded by demons. Though Zoraxzil's psionic powers let them see the trickery of the scene, they were incapacitated after eating the brain of a dretch, and Silent's mute nature meant he was unable to communicate easily. Though Graz'zt had set up Faraway in order to have the two sides kill each other, the rather simplistic storytelling and inch-deep setting made it easy enough for some light circumspection to carry the day, causing the demon-villagers to break character and try to kill everyone. Graz'zt was displeased by the heroes derailing his plot, but shrugged it off and invited them to come have a final showdown with him.

## Fate mantle

The Fate mantle was a psionic mantle that granted ardents and divine minds an array of psionics powers and abilities derived from the universal concept of knowledge.

Deities

Abilities
Those who followed the fate mantle could slightly alter the probability of their luck by expending a psionic focus.

The psychic aura exuded by divine minds of the fate mantle granted greater insight, allowing the manifester and nearby allies to alter the probability of their luck one or more times each, depending upon the the expertise of the divine mind.

Powers
Novice manifesters gained defensive and offensive precognitive abilities, as well as a clairvoyant sense. As a manifester progressed further they obtained greater precognition, the more enhanced clairvoyance of remote viewing, and further power to alter probabilities with Fate of One. Masters of this mantle obtained the power of metafaculty, allowing them to receive visions of a creature's whereabouts.

## False memories

False memories were the result of a person's recollections being altered, either by existing memories being modified or entirely new ones being implanted. False memories could be accompanied by a form of amnesia as the memories were imperfectly replaced.

Acquisition
 Modify memory could be used to alter existing memories or create new ones, in addition to sharpening or erasing them.
 Programmed amnnesia could cause amnesia to any level, and could add entirely new memories as desired to shape the target's personality. It was powerful enough to actively alter their personality, alignment, and values.
 Tailor memory could be used to insert a new memory without altering others.
 People with multiple personalities could develop elaborate false memories to create the imagined histories of their personality fragments.
 Those abducted into the realm of Bluetspur were often victim to mental techniques that gave them amnesia or false memories.

Effects

Altered memories were often imparted by manipulative spellcasters and psionicists that sought to subtly influence a person. By changing the details of a memory or adding a new one, a person's actions could be suitably redirected. However, most effects were unable to significantly directly alter a person's beliefs and personality.

False memories still needed to hold up to the conscious and unconscious scrutiny of the mind, and poorly-conceived alterations could be outright rejected with enough dissonance. Sufficiently nonsensical events that were physically impossible, wildly implausible, or completely out of character could be dismissed as bad dreams, intrusive thoughts, or drunken notions, even without them knowing they had been mentally intruded on. Even with the most powerful of memory-altering effects, if the memories were too dissonant or incomplete the victim could consciously hold them in suspicion and seek to investigate or cure them.

Notable Affected
 Alias was a construct clone, and retained no memories of her creation and early existence. In addition, she had elaborate implanted memories that gave her the false impression of having already lived a full life.
 Julith Harkspur was given false memories by Variance Amatick in order to implicate Keph Thingoleir as a dangerous enemy.
 Lyraene was given false memories by Variance as well in order to obfuscate the cause of her injuries. Instead of being tortured, she believed she was wounded in a fair fight.

## Fallen Crags

The Fallen Crags was an area of wild and dangerous rough rocky terrain and crags located at the heart of Icewind Dale.

Description
The Fallen Crags was the area that laid east of Maer Dualdon, southeast from Kelvin's Cairn, and northeast from Bryn Shander, placing it in the way of any caravans that moved from the Dwarven Valley to any of the Ten-Towns. The land of the Fallen Crags was littered with rocks, crevices, crags, and snowed-over canyons formed by tricking springs. Various species of local wildlife made traversing the Fallen Crags a challenge and the constant frigid fog that obscured the view across the Crags, made it a prime site for ambushes. Local bests, such as wolves, polar bears, and verbeegs, as well as snow goblins, were the primary danger for human and dwarvish traders alike. However, human bandits and ruffians were also known to camp in the area.

The Deep Rift, a canyon infested with snow spiders laid directly east of the Fallen Crags.

History

In Mirtul of the, as the Ten Towns and the Dwarven Valley were facing looming ancient evil. On her way to Targos, crossing the Fallen Crags, Baldryn Irontooth, a trader of Clan Firehammer, met "black-hearted swine and thieving scoundrels". She thought the group of humans not to be a danger, but she was wrong. The bandits quickly turned on her, surrounded, and made off with her mule and the shipment. Baldryn managed to escape unscathed and made it to Targos. The dwarf hoped to report the mugging and gather Targos militia to track the villains down, but ended up trapped in the city going into a lockdown – the result of an influx of refugees from the other Ten-Towns. Soon after, she met the Hero of Neverwinter, who sought to do business with Graendel Granitefist. Baldryn offered a generous reward for ending the brigands and returning the stolen cargo.

## Far Too Many Thieves

"Far Too Many Thieves" is a short story by Ed Greenwood, originally an outtake from a chapter in "Elminster: The Making of a Mage".

Summary
After forming the Velvet Hands thieves' gang in Hastarl, the young thief Elminster Aumar discovers the dead bodies of fellow thieves Klaern Blaenbar and his brother Othkyn Blaenbar. Elminster is shocked as he never thought they'd be killing, but Farl, guild leader, sees it as practical and needful, as they had probably joined the Moonclaws, a rival gang. their comrade Jhardin did it. Overnight, the Velvet Hands are ambushed by a number of Moonclaws; though they slay many rivals, the numbers of Velvet Hands thieves are reduced.

At dawn, Farl and Elminster discover Jhardin killed in his own home, realizing the Velvet Hands are now just them, Larrin, Tarth, and Tassabra. The two decide their gang need to hide, and then find out who the Moonclaws' master is.

## Fallen Oak Shrine

Fallen Oak Shrine was a woodland site found in the Lost Souls' Swamp in the King's Forest of Cormyr.The original faith or worship of the shrine goes undescribed.

Location

The shrine was in the midst of the cursed Lost Souls' Swamp, an at-best dismal patch of waterlogged woodlands, pools, and rock formations located on the northern edge of the King's Forest.

Description

The shrine, such as it was, was a set of partially submerged ancient megalithic ruins and shallow caves scattered through the swamp, and earned its name from the hollow trunk of an immense fallen oak tree that served as a bridge and tunnel from the rest of the swamp. The path to the fallen oak was marked by glowing posts set up by an enterprising wizard in order to make the journey safer.

History

Following the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon in the mid-1480s DR, Oriphaun Huntsilver scattered pieces of the Abyssal Planus Mechanus Nether Scroll along the High Road of Cormyr, hoping to confuse the Shadovar pursuit and give the Harpers time to catch up. He left one such piece in the care of the esteemed hermit Seamus, who kept it well hidden in the ruins—only for the artifact's emanations to instead attract the medusa Sshaastess. She chased him off and took the Scroll fragment, intent on studying and obtaining the powers within.

Not too long later, her new lair was well-decorated with the petrified forms of people and woodland creatures, and defended by her subservient earth elementals, crazed wolves, and maddened dryads. Her reign of localized terror ended when Harper reinforcements arrived and found Seamus, fought through her minions, and slew her. The Nether Scroll piece was found in the shallow cave where she stored her treasures (alongside the ironwood khopesh) and removed to a safer location before something worse showed up.

## Fane of Lolth (Shadowdark)

Interior
The Fane of Lolth was a natural cavern that could only be accessed via a hole in the ceiling of a lower, flooded passage. Every surface of the cavern was covered in spiders, which issued forth in a great geyser of spiders from the Font of Spiders, a planar touchstone situated in the room.

The Fane was the historical foundation of drow influence in the Shadowdark and Land Under Shadow (as Shadowdale was previously known).

The Fane was later raided by the rebels working to free Shadowdale from Zhentarim occupation.

## Faerim

Faerim was a sailor from Waterdeep in the mid–14 century DR.

History
Some time in the 1360s DR, Faerim came to be a captive aboard an infamous pirate ship, Scimitar of Fire, captained by Raurivyl Ornshield. Serving unwillingly as a crewman, he would be a witness to Raurivyl's voyages to the west.It is unknown how and why Faerim was a captive. In later sources, Raurivyl has a habit of taking important persons hostage and holding them for ransom, but a humble sailor seems less important. His position as crewman and ability to take treasure also suggest he had more freedom than a typical captive.

Seeking a desert island he might use as a safe haven and base, Raurivyl took the Scimitar of Fire westward on several voyages beyond the Gull Rocks. However, they found naught but a vast expanse of empty ocean. On three occasions they encountered the naval vessels of Evermeet, which rose out of the sea, hovered in the air, and tilted to direct their strange weaponry at the Scimitar. Via magical speaking horns, the elves ordered the pirates to leave. Sensibly, Captain Ornshield obeyed, but still the flying elven ships followed them until they were long gone.

Following months of such futile searching, Raurivyl returned to the Nelanther Isles. There, Faerim escaped, even taking some pirate treasure with him back to Waterdeep that he spent big with, along with tales of the elven ships. He insisted he was neither making it up, nor had been drinking.

## Fahd al Nisr

Fahd yn Sarsor al Nisr was a leader of the Rundeen mercantile organization, holding the position of a First Yrshelem, in the late 14 century DR.

Relationships
Fahd's son was Farid al Nisr, a wizard and member of the Harpers, despite the Rundeen's intense hostility toward Those Who Harp.It is unknown if Farid's membership of the Harpers is known to his father and the wider Rundeen or not.

History
In the spring of the, there was a surprise wedding between Fahd's son Farid and Syllia Adarbrent, daughter of House Adarbrent of Waterdeep (and also a Harper). Their families did not have long to be shocked before the pair sailed off to Maztica with no clue when they might return. However, House Adabrent were masters of the Adarbrent Shipping Company and the Horizon Armada league of shipowners, yet were struggling financially. Seizing an opportunity to expand and claim these northern shipping fortunes, Fahd contacted Lord Royus Adarbrent with offers of generous loans at very reasonable rates, if they but signed a contract with the Rundeen. Unfamiliar with the southern merchants, Royus considered this reluctantly and cautiously.
